# transaction.apply

Generated from the executable codecs. Do not edit this file.

Commit 1..32 ordered planning operations atomically, with typed creation aliases.

Tier: `core`. The result is `{data, meta}`; see [common envelopes and types](common.md).

| Input | Presence | Type and constraints |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `actor_id` | required | string; max UTF-8 bytes=96; 1..96 ASCII letters, digits, underscores or hyphens |
| `mutation_id` | required | string; max UTF-8 bytes=96; 1..96 ASCII letters, digits, underscores or hyphens |
| `operations` | required | array of `{method, params, as?}`; 1..32 ordered operations |
| `run_id` | optional | string; max UTF-8 bytes=96; 1..96 ASCII letters, digits, underscores or hyphens |
| `workspace_id` | required | string; max UTF-8 bytes=96; 1..96 ASCII letters, digits, underscores or hyphens |

Creation aliases are unique 1..96-byte ASCII IDs declared with `as`. `$alias` resolves only earlier creations in typed reference fields, never arbitrary text. Use `help METHOD` for each operation; the schema below lists the complete operation union.

For preconditions and a small example, run `workgraph help transaction.apply`. Use `--full` for the complete contracts below.

```xml
<workgraph_method name="transaction.apply" mode="mutation">
<summary><![CDATA[Commit 1..32 ordered planning operations atomically, with typed creation aliases.]]></summary>
```
